The current legislation of the Health and Food Safety Act aims to create the legal conditions for assigning staff of the Agency's veterinary border inspection service to the permanent service with a view to closing inspection posts of the veterinary medical border inspection service. This is an example of evaluating structure, slimming it, and still ensuring full performance. The current legislative innovation of the Food Safety and Consumer Protection Act is intended to extend the category of persons authorised to carry out official veterinary work. With this extension, an even better supply of official medical services can be made. We can help the Austrian farmers, who have been a guarantee of food supply for centuries in our country, to provide competent partners to our high standards, which we are very proud of and for which we enjoy high recognition in the population, also ensure long-term. I should like to present a few of the content of the budget accompanying law we are discussing today to a wider public in order to present in the discussion the positions we are taking accordingly. The 2009 Budgetary Accompanying Act includes, among other things, a number of culturally significant regulations, such as the increase in basic fees for the federal museums of € 8.5 million from 2009 onwards and also the increase in the basic fee for the Bundestheater by €3.5 million, which was a long-term demand but will now be fulfilled. This can not only improve the infrastructure of the houses. In the area of museums, this also creates financial scope for a targeted expansion of the collection, for example in the field of contemporary art. Furthermore, the redistribution of the funds released by changes in the KommAustria Act is extremely positive, which can increase, for example, the TV film promotion fund from € 7.5 million to € 13.5 million. This financial incentive for further co-productions will contribute to greater value-added and the preservation of high-quality and creative jobs in Austria and thus also to a significant strengthening of the Austrian film industry. Another change: € 1 million is intended to promote non-commercial broadcasting. These funds are to be allocated to 13 free radios and television stations within the framework of project funding, thus making an important contribution to a diverse and high-quality range of programmes and ensuring the diversity of views. In addition, the Digitalisation Fund will receive €0.5 million in future and €5 million in support of private broadcasting. Finally, I would also like to mention the increase in the liability framework in the SME Funding Act. The increase in the liability of the Austrian hotel and tourism bank from € 250 million to €500 million as well as the increase in the ceiling for the acceptance of liability in individual cases from € 2 million to € 4 million by 2010 are measures to reduce bottlenecks in financing. This can meet the financing needs of tourism and leisure entrepreneurs in the field of investment and operating resources. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to make two environmental amendments. For environmental promotion in Germany and abroad, a commitment framework for the years 2009 to 2013 is laid down by law of €90 million annually. Furthermore, the volume of funding to improve the quality of water within the meaning of the Water Framework Directive will also finance those structural measures in river basins to which the Federal Government is obliged to implement. All in all, there are new regulations and commitments in the given budget framework, which we believe form and represent a protective sign against the crisis. I'm going to ask you to do it. In this sense, I would like to ask for support for the decision-making of the budget accompanying laws. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-05-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00021_d8e21546	Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! High house! On the subject of e-voting, I think it should be considered, in principle, whether a regulation is at all the appropriate instrument for defining democratic elections and electoral modes. I believe that it would be worth including this in a legislative work, because we see that single approaches that do not comply with democratic principles do not produce the result we expect. On the topic of e-voting, of course, you also have to look at the international standard. The U.S. Department of Defense has tried to make e-voting a principle for its relatives in overseas, and has done some studies. All the studies that have been carried out in this regard have come to the conclusion that it is first of all not certain that it is not certain from the outside that it is suitable for the manipulation of the inside and that it also violates the right of secret choice, because, despite encryption, one can use the individual's electoral behaviour in particular. The English have also tried to introduce e-voting. The evaluation has also come to the same conclusion: it is not safe and does not comply with democratic principles either. Finland has also made an attempt to introduce e-voting. Hundreds of votes have disappeared there. And the evaluation carried out by the Administrative Court in Finland came to the conclusion that e-voting is not compatible with the basic democratic principles of the secret election, but that security is not sufficient as is necessary for elections. The Germans did not perform an e-voting, but they set up electoral computers, it was selected by computer. There, too, the Federal Constitutional Court has come to realize that, in particular, the electoral process and the results of the elections are not comprehensible to the public and individuals and therefore a review of the electoral process within the meaning of the democratic principles of Western democracies is not possible. It is therefore prohibited in this knowledge e-voting. We therefore see that international studies are clearly opposed to calling e-voting a currently permissible and reliable procedure. Finally, even in the presidential elections in America, e-voting was the last attempt in Florida. There was an examination of the Florida State University, which came to the following conclusion: Firstly, the manipulation door and gate are open, and secondly, the secret choice is certainly not guaranteed because conclusions on the individual's election behaviour are possible. [[Abg. May. Musiol: Why did you vote against this request?]] This request came a little late. We've been against e-voting for several years. [[Abg. May. Musiol: You could have made an application yourself!]] I've never expressed a different opinion before. [[Abg. May. Musiol: But the deeds were others!]] The reason was that this was actually filed very late. I therefore think that this is a problematic procedure, because I believe that electoral procedures should not be defined in a minister's regulation. That is why we need to make a legal regulation. That is the basic principle I call for elections. One must also be aware that one cannot compare one with one vote for “Dancing Stars” for the next four to five years or more, but that is a electoral act that needs to be made aware of where social policy decisions take place, and this electoral act should also be known to the individual as such and not be equated with electoral acts spread through television and where one chooses the most beautiful man or the best dancer or what I know. That, I think, does not take place at the same level. And for this reason, e-voting should also be rejected. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] I believe that the experiment, as you can see now, has gone wrong because, as the colleague said, the system has fallen and there have been false party designations. With the introduction of the letter election, a very decisive change in the direction of e-voting was made. The legislator, who has so far made protection of the principle of the secret election the matter of the state and has allowed the vote exclusively in election cells or by flying election commissions, gave the citizen the responsibility to protect his own election secret. This means that the legislator has explicitly made the decision of value that he considers the voters to be responsible enough to choose the place of the vote in such a way that they are not affected at the moment of the vote. This principle is also followed by e-voting. Another decision of value has been made, namely the decision of value, that the electoral principles that I have already referred to ensure not only the secret election, but also the general right to vote. We have given a stronger preference to the right to vote in general in favour of the secret right to vote. This is a decision of value which, of course, also plays a role in e-voting. I should also like to remind you that in 1995 the Constitutional Court acknowledged that different standards must be applied to the national elections and to the elections of interests. On the basis of this, the Constitutional Court has already made it possible to elect letters in the Chamber of Workers' Election and in the election of representatives of the Federal Republic of Germany. Finally, it should be noted that the Council of Europe – and this is really unwarranted – assumes that e-voting and compliance with the electoral principles I have already mentioned do not preclude each other in any way. This also applies to the secret right to vote. This is clear and clear from the recommendations of the Council of Europe on e-voting. It also recommends that the electoral process of the e-voting be declared neutral and impartial to voters. Unfortunately, this was not possible in the face of the numerous anti-e-voting campaigns in the ÖH election campaign. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-05-19-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00021_d8e22312	My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to briefly address the amendment to § 106 of the Civil Procedure Code. This section concerns the delivery of procedural documents. So far – I am talking about complaints here – these had to be delivered by means of RSa letters in their own hands. In future, at the request of the Minister of Justice, a replacement service is to be changed to RSb letters. This change on the part of the Ministry is justified by the equivalence of the protection of recipients, with a margin for recipients and with cost savings for justice. I was and still am very skeptical of this change, since it is procedurally introductory documents such as complaints, which concern the most important areas of human life such as living and working. An infringement of the time limit in this area, for example due to late delivery or even omission of the delivery of RSb letters, can, of course, have a threat to the person concerned in existence. For this reason, with my colleague Mr Donnerbauer, I will put forward a motion for a resolution calling on the Minister of Justice to evaluate the effects of this new regulation by the end of 2010 in order to come to an assessment and, if necessary, to return to the original self-handling in the field of anti-terrorism. With the introduction of the euro, food has been enormously expensive. Unfortunately, our farmers did not know anything about this development. However, the import/export figures are terrifying for me as an animal protector: Imports of live imports – these “beautiful” animal transports that travel across Europe –: 11 400 in 1999, 40 500 in 2007 – quadrupled; with pigs 95 000 in 1999, 848 000 in 2007. As I said, per capita consumption has fallen. In fact, meat exports increased from 215 200 to 256 000 in the same period in the case of cattle, by 20 percent, and in the case of pigmeat from 56 000 to 2.2 million, i.e. fortyfold. The suspicion is that the animals have to suffer, transported across Europe, only to be slaughtered here in order to get the Austrian quality stamp to be exported again. [[Abg. Ing. After the Health Check last year, all companies receiving more than €5,000 will be reduced to modulation. This means that money will be deducted and given into rural development. Those who receive € 5 000 are excluded here – that is 65 percent of the farms that do not experience a reduction. For everyone else, there is an increased reduction – i.e. modulation –: not 5 percent, as it was originally, but rising to 10 percent in 2013. Farms that receive more than €300 000 will experience a further 4 percent reduction. There is a base amount for mining and less-favoured farms: Up to 6 hectares of the same equipment, and then it becomes less for rising farms. That is to say, we have a degressive reduction in the compensatory allowance. In other words, the bigger the farm, the less he receives payments. Then, also in the normal area outside the mining and disadvantaged areas, we have size limits, reductions from 100 hectares. If a farm in Austria has more than 100 hectares, it experiences a reduction, then up to 300 hectares, up to 500 hectares and up to 1,000 hectares. There are limits on investment promotion – which is intended to make smaller businesses relatively better. But yes, there are also bigger farms who receive premiums because they do the service per hectare. That's what I wanted to say. And this system is intended to ensure that we have a wide-ranging agricultural agriculture in Austria.

I would like to mention a few of these gaps, which are also often found in the report, for example, that there are many years of procedures for advance payment, For example, if the performance of the father is to be determined in most cases. During this procedure, there is no payment; if the father opposes a judicial decision, the payment will be delayed again because there is a maintenance. The advance is based on the income of the dependant and not on the child's needs – that is, many children receive no or only a very small advance – that the advance payments end with the 18th. The child's year of life, regardless of whether or not the training is completed, and there are no advance payments for special needs such as dental clamps, country school weeks or training costs. We agreed, in principle, on the reform of maintenance rights in the Declaration of Government, because it is urgently necessary, and the proposals we are bringing in and discussing are covered by many of the public prosecutors' proposals. A very important point – including the government agreement – is that the maintenance advance should be paid as long as there is a claim for maintenance. This means that the 18-year-old daughter or the 18-year-old son is not forced to sue, which the mother or the youth welfare authority did not succeed, namely the enforcement of the maintenance payment by the father. Now it is about accelerating the system, closing gaps and simplifying them. We simply need faster procedures, because it would be useful to have a maintenance advance immediately if there is already a corresponding title that has been submitted to the court. We have to close gaps because it is a matter of ensuring that there is still no maintenance, for example, if the maintenance debtor is detained abroad or is in police custody at home. Or if a nurse – and that is, I find, especially unfortunate – to volunteer in therapy, then the children won't get any support! We must ensure that the child's need is at the forefront and not the performance of the dependant, and at least we need easier procedures because, for example, the research, which is often necessary in terms of the patient's stay, payment and performance, often takes months or years. We could agree on standards that, for example, it could be sufficient to carry out a question of the main association of social security funds and a question in the central reporting register, which would simplify and accelerate the matter and would at least make sense, because a reform of the maintenance right would reduce child poverty and secure child rights; I think that this is a concern to all of us. I am grateful for the dedicated work, for the concrete proposals and I am looking forward to further cooperation. - Thank you.

Other religious communities, for example, are governed by the law of recognition, such as the Witnesses of Jehovah and the Buddhists, and the law of Israel regulates funding for the Israelite congregation. If this request were a reality, the financing for the Evangelical, Catholic and the Old Catholic Church would be voluntary, while the Law of Recognition and the Law of Israel remained unaffected and would result in inequality between religious communities. Third argument: In your application you actually say very contradictoryly that you place on the one hand voluntaryity and, on the other hand, as a legislator, want to prescribe to the churches that they get a legal regulation on the use of the funds. [[Abg. May. Stadler: It is about the forced recovery of the contributions!]] This is absolutely contradictory, Mr Stadler, and you should actually know that the churches, of the Catholic Church, I know, of course, of the others, this possibility for contributors to determine the use of funds and the objective of the use of funds already exempt – in my diocese even very successful; I know that. Fourth point, Mr Stadler: I think, regardless of the obligations of the Concordat, that we have always discussed such issues with the religious communities and have never decided on the minds of the religious communities. Therefore, this request will not find our approval.
